_source line 9133 · 2026-05-21T03:59:11.795053+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\% on incorrect problems), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. More broadly, any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck, whether the channel is formal or neural. Three reusable diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail; practitioners should evaluate $C_0$ and SC accuracy first ($C_0 < 5\%$: SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$: error amplification risk). A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$), suggesting architectural diversity as the path forward. Open questions include extraction temperature sensitivity, scaling beyond 32B, and whether trained verifiers can close the NLI--oracle gap.
4

_source line 9135 · 2026-05-21T03:59:11.846032+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	The Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) confirms this scales with capability; thinking-mode degeneration ($\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) marks the upper bound. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. An alternative $\sigma(c, a)$ via Z3 semantic evaluation could in principle avoid degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), but faces circular dependency and converges at $\text{BR} = 4.97\times$.
5

[Director 指令] [Critical Polish] Discussion 扩展 + Intro 差异化加强

**背景**：Reviewer 模拟 R2 审稿发现 Discussion 仅 4 句话（~90 words），是 3.0→3.5 的最大障碍。这是当前 ROI 最高的修改，不需要新实验。

**任务 1（最高优先级）：Discussion 扩展到 3-4 段（0.75-1.0 页）**

扩展方向（全部基于现有数据，不要虚构）：

**段 1 — Self-verification 一般化**：
BR>1 不仅适用于 FOL extraction，也预测 NL self-verification 的失败。连接 Huang et al. 2024 和 Stechly et al. 2025：他们定性观察到 self-correction 无效，我们的 BR=4.97× 定量解释了为什么——self-extracted verification criteria 系统性偏向模型自身答案。"any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck"（目前只在 Conclusion 一句话，需要在 Discussion 展开论证）。

**段 2 — Test-time compute scaling 含义**：
K_eff=1.2-3.6 for nominal K=12 意味着增加 sampling budget 的收益急剧递减。QwQ-32B κ=0.91 (K_eff=1.09) 意味着 reasoning models 的 trace 近乎确定性。对 o1/R1 style 的 long reasoning chain + self-verification：更强的模型产生更一致的 traces，**降低**了同模型聚合的有效性。κ universality 跨 4 families × 3 methods（SC κ=0.44, Debate κ=0.15 但 acc -18.6pp, Diverse-Prompt κ=0.40）表明 "same-model trace diversity has a floor"。

**段 3 — Independence-accuracy tradeoff 的 ensemble 理论意义**：
展开 Krogh & Vetter 1994 引用。Debate 成功打破 κ（0.44→0.15, K_eff 2.0→4.4）但 accuracy 崩塌，这不是 implementation 问题而是 ensemble learning 的基本 tension：同一模型不能同时输出一致且独立的 traces。Cross-architecture escape 之所以 work（Spearman ρ=0.970），正是因为 DeBERTa 和 decoder-only LLM 有结构性不同的 error profiles。

**段 4 — Practitioner implications**：
连接 Conclusion 的 C0/SC 诊断到 broader 决策：C0<5% → 不值得跑 SICA；SC≤40% → error amplification risk；中等 SC + 有外部 verifier → cross-architecture combo 值得尝试。同时提及 Z3 semantic dedup 的 positive signal (p=0.013) 作为"mechanism is sound, extraction is bottleneck"的额外证据。

**页数预算**：当前 Discussion < 0.25 页。扩展到 ~0.75 页。如果超 8 页限制，从 §4 Experiments setup 或 §5 Analysis 中压缩移入 appendix（setup 细节最容易压缩）。

**任务 2：Intro self-correction differentiation 加强**

在 introduction.tex:25（"Z3 introduces a sound, non-neural channel"）之后加 2-3 句，从 assertion 升级为 argument：

参考句式（按需调整）：
"Unlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints: Z3 can detect inconsistencies between constraints, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger feedback loop still fails—Z3 rejects only 3.4% of constraints (§4.2) because constraints are individually valid yet collectively biased—reveals a failure mode invisible to prior work: the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it's expressed)."

**任务 3：两个快速补强**

A. **experiments.tex:66 附近加 4-seed anti-cherry-pick 句**："Four-seed replication shows that intermediate-SC positive trends reflect seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative Δ, and the aggregate (+3.43pp) does not reach significance (p=0.092)."

B. **Z3 semantic dedup 扩展**：analysis.tex 中将当前一句话扩展为 2-3 句："Z3-based semantic deduplication's positive signal confirms that the formal verification channel provides genuine value when redundancy is reduced; the bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality and independence of input constraints."

**硬约束**：主文 ≤8 页（Limitations 豁免）。修完编译确认。

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	The Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) confirms this scales with capability; thinking-mode degeneration ($\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) marks the upper bound. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. An alternative $\sigma(c, a)$ via Z3 semantic evaluation could in principle avoid degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}), but faces circular dependency and converges at $\text{BR} = 4.97\times$.
5

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
13	
14	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
15	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
16	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyon

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models on three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations across ${\sim}$1,250 unique instances. Beyond accuracy comparisons, we conduct diagnostic analyses of symbolic confirmation bias and process-level trace independence (\S\ref{sec:analysis}).
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$; extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$. Z3 timeout: 10s. $\alpha{=}0.5$. Statistical significance via McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs from 10,000 resamples for $n \geq 200$.
14	
15	\paragraph{Computational cost.} On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($K{=}12$), SICA takes $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock time; constraint extraction ($K$ additional LLM calls) accounts for 76\% of overhead, Z3 solving is negligible (${<}0.1\%$).
16	
17	
18	\subsection{Main Results}
19	\label{sec:main-results}
20	
21	Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline. We identify three empirical regimes (thresholds observation-driven, not theoretically derived). SICA effectiveness depends sharply on this accuracy regime.

1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	We test the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ produces degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\% on incorrect problems), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. More broadly, any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck. Three reusable diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail; practitioners should evaluate $C_0$ and SC accuracy first ($C_0 < 5\%$: SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$: error amplification risk). A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$), pointing to architectural diversity. Open questions include temperature sensitivity, scaling beyond 32B, and whether trained verifiers can close the NLI--oracle gap.
4

1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is a dataset-specific hyperparameter ($w{=}5$ yields $+10.67$\,pp on Mistral-7B but only $+1.67$\,pp n.s.\ on LLaMA-3.1-8B), and higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\%) and performs poorly on \textsc{Unknown} (3.5\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.
10

1	\section{Background \& Setup}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}
5	\label{sec:condorcet}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \to \infty$ \citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = \frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \cdot \kappa}.
11	\end{equation}
12	In our experiments (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\rho$) in \citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This \emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.
13	
14	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
15	\label{sec:pipeline}
16	
17	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
18	
19	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K

  "old_string": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nThe Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\\times$ LLaMA) confirms this scales with capability; thinking-mode degeneration ($\\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) marks the upper bound. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. An alternative $\\sigma(c, a)$ via Z3 semantic evaluation could in principle avoid degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), but faces circular dependency and converges at $\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$.",
  "new_string": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). This mechanism extends beyond formal extraction. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback, but their analyses remain qualitative. Our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) supplies a quantitative explanation: self-extracted verification criteria systematically confirm the generating model's answer because extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints despite the model erring on ${\\sim}$44\\% of problems, confirming the bias is semantic---what gets extracted---rather than syntactic. The gradient $\\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether the channel is formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute scaling.}\n$K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling budgets beyond a dozen traces yield diminishing independent information. The pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode produces $\\kappa = 0.91$ ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), indicating near-deterministic traces. For reasoning models in the o1/R1 paradigm \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, this creates a scaling paradox: stronger models produce more consistent traces, \\emph{reducing} the effectiveness of same-model aggregation precisely when it should matter most. The $\\kappa$ floor holds across four model families and three aggregation methods---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), while Debate achieves $\\kappa = 0.15$ only by collapsing accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Same-model trace diversity has a structural ceiling that no prompting strategy can break.\n\n\\paragraph{Ensemble diversity.}\nThe independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: ensemble performance requires members that are individually accurate \\emph{and} mutually diverse, but a single model cannot simultaneously produce consistent and independent outputs. Debate demonstrates this: it raises $K_{\\text{eff}}$ from ${\\sim}1.8$ to ${\\sim}4.5$ by forcing adversarial exchanges, but sacrifices accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) because diversity-inducing perturbations to a single model's generation process degrade its predictions rather than introducing genuinely independent reasoning paths. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$, $p < 0.001$) precisely because encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only LLMs possess structurally different error profiles, satisfying the diversity condition without sacrificing individual accuracy. If traces do not faithfully reflect the model's reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction from opaque models.\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}\nTwo diagnostics predict when symbolic augmentation will fail before running the full pipeline: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA produces the same output as SC (degenerate regime); SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp on ProofWriter). For intermediate-SC models with access to an external verifier, cross-architecture combination is viable---gain magnitude is predicted by the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$), providing a principled selection criterion. An alternative scoring function $\\sigma(c, a)$ via Z3 semantic evaluation could in principle bypass degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}), but faces the same circular dependency: semantically evaluating constraint--answer alignment requires the model to judge its own outputs. Z3 semantic deduplication yields a small but significant improvement ($p = 0.013$; \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), confirming the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced; the bottleneck is not verification but extraction quality. The same mechanism that fails under self-extraction succeeds with oracle constraints ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp), bounding the prize for better extraction methods."
}

_source line 9179 · 2026-05-21T04:42:10.180568+00:00_

24	Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck persists at the 7B--32B scale.
25	Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case. We show that even formal verification inherits the model's bias, revealing process-level homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.
26	
27	Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \S\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
28	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp; other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).

    "new_string": "Z3 introduces a \\emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \\emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.\nUnlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints: Z3 can detect logical inconsistencies, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because constraints are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed)."
  }
}

65	
66	None of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation (uncorrected McNemar $p < 0.05$): Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} ($\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for 5 conditions; 5 show genuine differences (4 negative); 6 are underpowered (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). We trace the failure to three mechanisms in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\S\ref{sec:remediation}.
67	
68	
69	\subsection{Oracle Control}
70	\label{sec:oracle}
71	
72	Oracle variants replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: logical formulae from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \times 10^{-7}$), and $+11.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\% (ProofWriter) of problems. The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted result ($+3.43$\,pp) captures only 11.5\% of its oracle ceiling, with ${\sim}$88\% lost to extraction quality.
73

61	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
62	\label{sec:diagnostics}
63	
64	Further diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\sim}$10\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant improvement ($p = 0.013$), indicating that the formal verification mechanism itself adds value---but this gain is overwhelmed by confirmation bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$).

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models on three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations across ${\sim}$1,250 unique instances. Beyond accuracy comparisons, we conduct diagnostic analyses of symbolic confirmation bias and process-level trace independence (\S\ref{sec:analysis}).
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$; extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$. Z3 timeout: 10s. $\alpha{=}0.5$. Statistical significance via McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs from 10,000 resamples for $n \geq 200$.
14	
15	\paragraph{Computational cost.} On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($K{=}12$), SICA takes $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock time; constraint extraction ($K$ additional LLM calls) accounts for 76\% of overhead, Z3 solving is negligible (${<}0.1\%$).
16	
17	
18	\subsection{Main Results}
19	\label{sec:main-results}
20

36	
37	\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:
38	\begin{equation}
39	\label{eq:degenerate}
40	\text{score}(a) \approx \sum_{\substack{c \in \mathcal{C}^* \\ \text{source}(c) \to a}} w_c,
41	\end{equation}
42	equivalent to majority voting. We show in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.
43	
44	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\{q : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}| / |\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC. The penalty weight $\alpha = 0.5$ is not a critical hyperparameter: accuracy varies by at most $0.49$\,pp across $\alpha \in \{0.0, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\}$ on Mistral-7B FOLIO.
45	
46	\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}
47	\label{sec:mirage}
48	
49	We hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \emph{Bias Ratio}:
50	\begin{equation}
51	\label{eq:mirage}
52	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
53	\end{equation}
54	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Toge

_source line 9207 · 2026-05-21T04:46:45.668666+00:00_

42	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
43	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
44	
45	Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\% of constraints on average, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}).
46	
47	The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts logical constraints consistent with it. Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% zero cross-answer variable overlap), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all and aggregation reduces to weighted majority voting. Confirmation bias generalizes across families: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.93\times$ (Qwen-14B), and $2.88\times$ (LLaMA), all exceeding $1.0$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Bias intensifies along reasoning steps: late-step constraints (step $\geq 7$) exhibit 27.2\% conclusion leakage vs.\ 22.0\% for early steps (Fisher $p = 3 \times 10^{-6}$); SICA restricted to late-only constraints degrades by $-10.3$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}).
48	
49	
50	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
51	\label{sec:process-kappa}
52	
53	Answer-level $\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
54	\begin{equation}
55	\label{eq:process-sim}
56	\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i^q, \mathbf{v}_j^q),
57	\end{equation}
58	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answ
…[truncated 1759 chars]

_source line 9219 · 2026-05-21T04:48:25.535599+00:00_

22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract logical constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	We evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations with four-seed replication (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck persists at the 7B--32B scale.
25	Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.
26	Unlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints: Z3 can detect logical inconsistencies, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because constraints are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).
27

_source line 9231 · 2026-05-21T04:51:32.838674+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}
5	The Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). This mechanism extends beyond formal extraction. \citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback, but their analyses remain qualitative. Our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) supplies a quantitative explanation: self-extracted verification criteria systematically confirm the generating model's answer because extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints despite the model erring on ${\sim}$44\% of problems, confirming the bias is semantic---what gets extracted---rather than syntactic. The gradient $\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether the channel is formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
6	
7	\paragraph{Test-time compute scaling.}
8	$K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling budgets beyond a dozen traces yield diminishing independent information. The pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode produces $\kappa = 0.91$ ($K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$), indicating near-deterministic traces. For reasoning models in the o1/R1 paradigm \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, this creates a scaling paradox: stronger models produce more consistent traces, \emph{reducing} the effectiveness of same-model aggregation precisely when it should matter most. The $\kappa$ floor holds across four model families and three aggregation methods---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ (Table~

  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
  "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). This extends beyond formal extraction. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: self-extracted criteria systematically confirm the generating model's answer because extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. The gradient $\\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether the channel is formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}\n$K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\\text{eff}}$ from ${\\sim}1.8$ to ${\\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$) precisely because encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only LLMs have structurally different error profiles. If traces do not faithfully reflect reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction from opaque models.\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}\nTwo diagnostics predict failure before running the full pipeline: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp on ProofWriter). For intermediate-SC models with an external verifier, gain magnitude tracks the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$), providing a principled selection criterion. Z3 semantic deduplication's positive signal ($p = 0.013$; \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}) confirms the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced; the bottleneck is extraction quality, as the same mechanism succeeds with oracle constraints ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n"
}

    "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}\nThe Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). This extends beyond formal extraction. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \\citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: self-extracted criteria systematically confirm the generating model's answer because extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. The gradient $\\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether the channel is formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}\n$K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \\citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\\text{eff}}$ from ${\\sim}1.8$ to ${\\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$) precisely because encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only LLMs have structurally different error profiles. If traces do not faithfully reflect reasoning \\citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction from opaque models.\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}\nTwo diagnostics predict failure before running the full pipeline: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp on ProofWriter). For intermediate-SC models with an external verifier, gain magnitude tracks the capability gap (Spearman $\\rho = 0.970$), providing a principled selection criterion. Z3 semantic deduplication's positive signal ($p = 0.013$; \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}) confirms the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced; the bottleneck is extraction quality, as the same mechanism succeeds with oracle constraints ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n"
  }
}
```

_source line 9235 · 2026-05-21T04:51:55.311675+00:00_

49	We hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \emph{Bias Ratio}:
50	\begin{equation}
51	\label{eq:mirage}
52	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
53	\end{equation}
54	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Together with the oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}), BR bounds improvement: self-extracted constraints capture at most a $1/\text{BR}$ fraction of the oracle ceiling ($\Delta_{\max} \lesssim 6.0$\,pp for Mistral-7B, $\lesssim 3.6$\,pp for Qwen-14B; observed mean across conditions: $+2.12$\,pp). If chain-of-thought traces are unfaithful representations of the model's actual reasoning process \citep{turpin2024language}, self-extracted constraints inherit representation bias rather than reasoning content---making the mirage an inherent property of extraction from opaque models, not merely a quality issue. Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\S\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
55

_source line 9239 · 2026-05-21T04:52:05.543983+00:00_

49	
50	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
51	\label{sec:process-kappa}
52	
53	Answer-level $\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
54	\begin{equation}
55	\label{eq:process-sim}
56	\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i^q, \mathbf{v}_j^q),
57	\end{equation}
58	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers. This generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\kappa$ gap ranges from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing that process-$\kappa$ uncovers hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. Interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.
59	
60

_source line 9243 · 2026-05-21T04:52:18.318933+00:00_

24	Prior work establishes that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, but concerns natural-language self-correction. Z3 introduces a \emph{sound}, non-neural channel that \emph{guarantees} logical consistency given well-formed constraints, making self-extraction under formal verification a non-trivial test case.
25	Unlike natural-language self-correction, formal extraction produces machine-verifiable constraints: Z3 can detect logical inconsistencies, making extraction errors in principle detectable. That this stronger channel still fails---Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) because constraints are individually valid yet collectively biased---reveals that the bias is semantic (what gets extracted) rather than syntactic (how it is expressed).
26	
27	Oracle controls confirm the mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp Mistral-7B, $+14.22$\,pp Qwen-14B on FOLIO, $p < 0.001$; \S\ref{sec:oracle}), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling.
28	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ to 0.15 but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp; other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling across three families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).

_source line 9269 · 2026-05-21T04:54:37.297322+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\% on incorrect problems), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. More broadly, any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck. Three reusable diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail; practitioners should evaluate $C_0$ and SC accuracy first ($C_0 < 5\%$: SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$: error amplification risk). A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$), pointing to architectural diversity. Open questions include temperature sensitivity, scaling beyond 32B, and whether trained verifiers can close the NLI--oracle gap.
4

_source line 9283 · 2026-05-21T04:55:30.434836+00:00_

1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures on
…[truncated 367 chars]

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}
5	The Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). This extends beyond formal extraction. \citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: self-extracted criteria systematically confirm the generating model's answer because extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. The gradient $\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether the channel is formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
6	
7	\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}
8	$K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\text{eff}}$ from ${\sim}1.8$ to ${\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. _source line 9287 · 2026-05-21T04:55:30.587370+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	We identify the Symbolic Mirage: self-extracted logical constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\% on incorrect problems), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the failure is in self-extraction. More broadly, any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model risks the same bottleneck. Three diagnostics---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---predict when trace-aggregation will fail ($C_0 < 5\%$: SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$: error amplification). A cross-architecture proof-of-concept escapes the mirage on two of four generators (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$). Open questions include temperature sensitivity, scaling beyond 32B, and closing the NLI--oracle gap.
4

[Director 指令] [Patch — V14 三路并行, 3 items]

**Patch 1 (Route A, P0): §3 末尾加 Capability Gap Corollary**

在 Proposition 1 (BR ceiling) 之后，加入以下 Corollary + sketch proof：

---

\begin{corollary}[Capability Gap Condition for Cross-Architecture Escape]
\label{cor:capability_gap}
Let $G$ be a generator producing $K$ traces with self-consistency accuracy $\text{SC}_G$, and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\text{acc}$ whose error profile is partially independent from $G$ (inter-system correlation $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$, where $\rho_{GG}$ is the intra-generator trace correlation). The combined ensemble $E = \{\text{SC}_1, \ldots, \text{SC}_K, V\}$ achieves:
\[
\Delta_\text{combo} \geq \Delta_\text{SC} \quad \iff \quad V_\text{acc} \gtrsim \text{SC}_G - \epsilon(w_V, K)
\]
where $\epsilon$ decreases with verifier weight $w_V$ and increases with $K$. The gain $\Delta_\text{combo} - \Delta_\text{SC}$ is a monotonically increasing function of the capability gap $(V_\text{acc} - \text{SC}_G)$.
\end{corollary}

\textit{Proof sketch.} From Proposition~1, the SC ceiling $\Delta_\text{max}^\text{SC}$ is bounded by bias ratio $\text{BR}$. Adding verifier $V$ with $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$ increases the effective number of independent signals from $K_\text{eff}^\text{SC}$ to:
\[
K_\text{eff}^\text{combo} = K_\text{eff}^\text{SC} + w_V \cdot \frac{1 - \rho_{GV}}{1 - \rho_{GG}}
\]
This reduces the effective bias ratio: $\text{BR}_\text{combo} = \text{BR}_\text{SC} \cdot \frac{K_\text{eff}^\text{SC}}{K_\text{eff}^\text{combo}}$. However, the verifier's vote shifts the majority only when its signal is accurate; when $V_\text{acc} < \text{SC}_G$, the verifier's incorrect votes counteract the accuracy gain from independence. The net effect follows a sigmoid: gain $\approx L / (1 + e^{-k(V_\text{acc} - \text{SC}_G + \epsilon)})$ where $L, k$ depend on $K$ and $w_V$. Empirically, our 8-point cross-architecture evaluation yields Spearman $\rho = 0.970$ ($p < 0.001$) between capability gap and combo gain, with sigmoid $R^2 = 0.875$. $\square$

---

这段放在 §3 最后一个 Proposition 之后，§4 之前。

**Patch 2 (Route C simplified): Discussion/Conclusion 加 1 段 thinking-mode connection**

在 Discussion section 加 1 段（D116 user-approved framing，softened per D122）：

"Our findings illuminate why the recent paradigm shift from test-time aggregation to trained reasoning internalization (e.g., o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking mode) yields outsized gains. Single-trace accuracy of Qwen3-14B in thinking mode (85.42\%) already exceeds the theoretical ceiling of any Mistral-7B aggregation scheme ($\Delta_\text{max} \approx 57\%$, Proposition~1). Rather than extracting and aggregating external symbolic structure --- which inherits the generator's biases --- these models internalize the verification process during training, bypassing the independence bottleneck entirely. The Capability Gap Corollary (Corollary~\ref{cor:capability_gap}) formalizes this boundary: test-time aggregation helps only when verifier capability exceeds generator capability, a condition that trained reasoning renders unnecessary."

**Patch 3: 不动 Abstract/Intro**（已有 D122 softened sentences，Route C 补在 Discussion 不升级 claim 强度）。

**页数**：Corollary ~0.3 页 + Discussion 段 ~0.1 页 = +0.4 页。如需空间从 §4 继续压缩。

1	\section{Background \& Setup}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}
5	\label{sec:condorcet}
6	
7	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \to \infty$ \citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:
8	\begin{equation}
9	\label{eq:effk}
10	K_{\text{eff}} = \frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \cdot \kappa}.
11	\end{equation}
12	In our experiments (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\text{eff}} \approx 1.2\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. $K_{\text{eff}}$ should be interpreted as an ordinal diagnostic---ranking which conditions have genuine vote diversity---rather than as a literal effective sample size. Fleiss' $\kappa$ is a chance-corrected agreement measure, not identical to the ICC ($\rho$) in \citeauthor{kish1965survey}'s (\citeyear{kish1965survey}) original formula; we validated their monotonic relationship empirically (mean pairwise $r = 0.625$ across model families). This \emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.
13	
14	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
15	\label{sec:pipeline}
16	
17	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
18	
19	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}
5	The Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). This extends beyond formal extraction. \citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: self-extracted criteria systematically confirm the generating model's answer because extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. The gradient $\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether the channel is formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
6	
7	\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}
8	$K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\text{eff}}$ from ${\sim}1.8$ to ${\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$) precisely because encod

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\S\ref{sec:analysis}).
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.
14	
15	
16	\subsection{Main Results}
17	\label{sec:main-results}
18	
19	Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline. We identify three empirical regimes (thresholds observation-driven, not theoretically derived). SICA effectiveness depends sharply on this accuracy regime.

1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	We test the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ produces degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).

_source line 9318 · 2026-05-21T05:04:13.714326+00:00_

1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is a dataset-specific hyperparameter ($w{=}5$ yields $+10.67$\,pp on Mistral-7B but only $+1.67$\,pp n.s.\ on LLaMA-3.1-8B), and higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ ($p < 0.001$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. Per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\%) and performs poorly on \textsc{Unknown} (3.5\% accuracy), as NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	The 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral sha

Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). [Director 指令] [Patch — V14 DRC Integration, P0 优先级] DRC 数据已出，立即集成。这是 R2 novelty 的核心回应。

**Patch 1: §6 末尾新增 sub-section "Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing"**

结构（约 0.5-0.7 页）：

\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}
\label{sec:drc}

**段落 1 (Motivation, 3-4 句)**:
Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes the NLI verifier on every question, but high-agreement questions (where SC traces already converge) gain little from external verification. We propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC): a per-question adaptive routing mechanism that invokes the NLI verifier only when SC trace agreement falls below a threshold $\tau$.

**段落 2 (Method, 3-4 句)**:
For each question $Q$, we compute the SC agreement score $\kappa_Q$ as the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer. When $\kappa_Q \geq \tau$, we use the SC majority vote directly; when $\kappa_Q < \tau$ (disagreement indicates difficulty), we invoke DeBERTa-large-mnli and apply the weighted SC+NLI combo (w=3). The threshold $\tau$ controls the cost--accuracy trade-off.

**Table (4 rows + 2 baselines)**:

\begin{table}[t]
\centering
\small
\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier). DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 93\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\% of questions.}
\label{tab:drc}
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
Method & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\
\midrule
SC only ($\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\% & 0/600 & 0\% \\
DRC $\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\% & 65/600 & 10.8\% \\
\textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{45.33\%} & \textbf{233/600} & \textbf{38.8\%} \\
DRC $\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\% & 455/600 & 75.8\% \\
Full combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\% & 600/600 & 100\% \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

**段落 3 (Analysis, 3-4 句)**:
DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 93\% of the full-combo accuracy gain ($+6.00$pp out of $+6.67$pp over SC) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\% of questions. The remaining 61.2\% of questions --- those where SC traces show high agreement --- are correctly resolved by majority vote alone. This confirms that NLI verification is most valuable precisely where SC is uncertain, consistent with the Capability Gap Hypothesis: the verifier adds value when the generator's internal consensus is weak.

**Figure**: 如果 Worker 生成了 `pareto_front.pdf`，从服务器下载到 `docs/paper/figures/` 并加入。Caption: "DRC Pareto frontier: accuracy vs. NLI invocation rate. $\tau{=}0.7$ (star) achieves the efficiency knee."。如果 figure 不可用，table 已足够，跳过 figure。

**Patch 2: Abstract 加 1 句**

在现有 NLI escape 句之后加：
"We further propose Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC), which adaptively invokes cross-architecture verification only on low-agreement questions, recovering 93\% of full-combo gain at 39\% of verification cost."

**Patch 3: §1 contributions list 加第 4 条**

在现有 contributions 列表末尾加：
"(iv) \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)}: a Pareto-efficient adaptive routing method that selectively invokes cross-architecture verification based on per-question trace agreement, achieving near-optimal accuracy at a fraction of the cost."

**页数**：DRC subsection ~0.6 页。如 main text 超 8 页，从 §4 SICA exhaustion 压缩（合并多个 negative direction 描述为 1 段概述 + appendix table）。

_source line 9356 · 2026-05-21T05:08:22.176752+00:00_

1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test this with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs into \textsc{entailment}, \textsc{contradiction}, or \textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via weight $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+1.96$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\,pp ($p = 0.013$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary; $w$ is dataset-specific ($w{=}5$: $+10.67$\,pp Mistral-7B but $+1.67$\,pp n.s.\ LLaMA-3.1-8B). After BH correction ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adj.\ $p = 0.038$); FOLIO comparisons are non-significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	Encoder-only DeBERTa (406M) and decoder-only Mistral (7B) share no architecture or training data, satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). _source line 9358 · 2026-05-21T05:08:22.529627+00:00_

1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant effect after FDR control (Benjamini--Hochberg, $m{=}16$); TOST equivalence tests (margin $\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for five conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.46$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-seven same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures on
…[truncated 367 chars]

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
13	
14	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
15	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
16	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyon
…[truncated 5948 chars]

  "new_string": "{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$SC from combo trace set; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports 75.17\\% (same seed, different server).}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes the NLI verifier on every question, but high-agreement questions---where SC traces already converge---gain little from external verification. We propose \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)}: a per-question adaptive routing mechanism that invokes the NLI verifier only when SC trace agreement falls below a threshold $\\tau$.\n\nFor each question $q$, we compute the SC agreement score as the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer. When agreement $\\geq \\tau$, we use the SC majority vote directly; when agreement $< \\tau$ (disagreement indicates difficulty), we invoke DeBERTa-large-mnli and apply the weighted combo ($w{=}3$). The threshold $\\tau$ controls the cost--accuracy trade-off.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC) on Mistral-7B ProofWriter ($n{=}600$, $K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier, $w{=}3$). DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ achieves 90\\% of full-combo accuracy gain while invoking NLI on only 39\\% of questions.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & Accuracy & NLI Calls & Cost Ratio \\\\\n\\midrule\nSC only ($\\tau{=}0.3$) & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.5$ & 41.50\\% & 65/600 & 10.8\\% \\\\\n\\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} \\\\\nDRC $\\tau{=}0.9$ & 45.83\\% & 455/600 & 75.8\\% \\\\\nFull combo ($w{=}3$) & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nDRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of the full-combo accuracy gain ($+6.00$\\,pp out of $+6.67$\\,pp over SC) while invoking NLI on only 38.8\\% of questions. The remaining 61.2\\%---where SC traces show high agreement---are correctly resolved by majority vote alone. This confirms that NLI verification is most valuable where SC is uncertain, consistent with Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap}: the verifier adds value when the generator's internal consensus is weak.\n"
}

_source line 9384 · 2026-05-21T05:13:31.829658+00:00_

55	
56	\begin{proposition}[Self-Extraction Ceiling]\label{prop:ceiling}
57	For generator $M$ with $\text{BR}(M) > 1$ and oracle improvement $\Delta_\text{oracle}$, self-extracted constraints improve SC by at most $\Delta_\text{max}^\text{self} \lesssim \Delta_\text{oracle} / \text{BR}(M)$.
58	\end{proposition}
59	
60	\begin{corollary}[Capability Gap Condition]\label{cor:capability_gap}
61	Let $G$ be a generator with SC accuracy $\text{SC}_G$ and let $V$ be an external verifier with standalone accuracy $V_\text{acc}$ and inter-system correlation $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$. Combo gain $\Delta_\text{combo} - \Delta_\text{SC}$ is monotonically increasing in the capability gap $(V_\text{acc} - \text{SC}_G)$.
62	\end{corollary}
63	
64	\textit{Proof sketch.} From Proposition~\ref{prop:ceiling}, the SC ceiling is bounded by $\text{BR}$. Adding $V$ with $\rho_{GV} < \rho_{GG}$ increases effective independent signals: $K_\text{eff}^\text{combo} = K_\text{eff}^\text{SC} + w_V \cdot (1 - \rho_{GV})/(1 - \rho_{GG})$, reducing the effective bias ratio. However, when $V_\text{acc} < \text{SC}_G$, incorrect verifier votes counteract independence gains. The net effect follows a sigmoid in the capability gap. Empirically, our cross-architecture evaluation yields Spearman $\rho = 0.970$ ($p < 0.001$) with sigmoid $R^2 = 0.875$. $\square$

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{Self-verification generalization.}
5	The Symbolic Mirage operates through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}), while answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). This extends beyond formal extraction. \citet{huang2024selfcorrect} and \citet{stechly2025selfverification} observe that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why: self-extracted criteria systematically confirm the generating model's answer because extraction and generation share the same distributional biases. The gradient $\text{BR} > 1$ across three families predicts that any self-verification pipeline deriving checking criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether the channel is formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
6	
7	\paragraph{Test-time scaling and ensemble diversity.}
8	$K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information, and the pattern intensifies with capability: Qwen3 thinking mode ($\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$) produces near-deterministic traces, creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style reasoning models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. The $\kappa$ floor is universal---all non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}), confirming a structural ceiling. This instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}: Debate raises $K_{\text{eff}}$ from ${\sim}1.8$ to ${\sim}4.5$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp, $p < 0.001$), because diversity-inducing perturbations degrade predictions rather than introducing independent reasoning. Cross-architecture escape succeeds (Spearman $\rho = 0.970$) precisely because encod

{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
  "old_string": "Our findings illuminate why the paradigm shift from test-time aggregation to trained reasoning internalization (e.g., o1, DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3 thinking mode) yields outsized gains. Qwen3-14B in thinking mode achieves $86\\%$ on FOLIO, already exceeding the theoretical self-extraction ceiling of Mistral-7B (${\\sim}62\\%$; Proposition~\\ref{prop:ceiling} with $\\text{BR} = 4.97$). Rather than extracting and aggregating external symbolic structure---which inherits the generator's biases---these models internalize verification during training, bypassing the independence bottleneck entirely. Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap} formalizes this boundary: test-time aggregation helps only when verifier capability exceeds generator capability, a condition that trained reasoning renders unnecessary.",

Rather than extracting external symbolic structure that inherits generator biases, these models internalize verification during training, bypassing the bottleneck. Corollary~\\ref{cor:capability_gap} formalizes this: test-time aggregation helps only when verifier capability exceeds generator capability."
